# Decisions

Record every decision the spec does not cover: date, decision, reason, alternatives considered.

| Date | Decision | Reason | Alternatives |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-29 | Build our own hub instead of forking an existing orchestrator | Owner wants full ownership of the core | Fork Open Session, Emdash or Vibe Kanban |
| 2026-09-29 | Drive agents over ACP | One protocol for Claude, Codex and Kimi, no terminal scraping | Wrapping each CLI's own non-interactive mode |
| 2026-09-29 | Local memory store that never calls a model | Company accounts are CLI subscriptions, not API keys | Mem0, Graphiti (both need an API key for extraction) |
| 2026-09-29 | v1 supports Claude Code and Codex only. Kimi removed. Tools are entries in a registry | Owner uses Claude and Codex now; may add OpenCode, Cursor or others later without touching the rest of the hub | Keep Kimi; hardcode each tool |
| 2026-09-29 | Accounts sign in with the tool's login or with an API key. Keys live in `secrets.age` and go only to runs on that account | Owner may use API keys when needed | Subscription logins only |
| 2026-09-29 | Several workspace roots picked by the owner, not a fixed `~/Work`. One `tasks_dir` (default `<first root>/.hub`) holds all task folders | Owner keeps projects in more than one place. One tasks folder keeps a task whose repos span roots in one home | Single fixed root; task folder per root |
| 2026-09-29 | Roots are mounted through a generated `docker-compose.override.yml`. Changing roots needs `make up` again | Compose cannot loop over a list and mounts are fixed at start. Mounting only the roots keeps agents away from the rest of the disk | Mount all of `$HOME` once (instant changes, wider exposure) |
| 2026-09-29 | Root agents can create tasks, use any attached MCP server, edit `hub.yaml`, and move or rename project folders. Every change is a proposal the owner approves | Owner wants root agents to organize projects, with approval | Read-only root agents; auto-apply |
| 2026-09-29 | Optional decision provider (rules, Laya, Jev) for typed decisions: routing, model picking for `model: auto` agents, memory dedupe, injection warnings | Jev and Laya answer typed questions fast and cheap, but cannot write code or speak ACP, so they sit beside agents, not in place of them | Use an LLM agent for every small decision; skip decision models |
| 2026-09-29 | Jev is off by default | It is a hosted API, so task text leaves the machine | On by default when a key is present |
| 2026-09-29 | The product is called majhi | Owner's choice | Work Hub |
| 2026-09-29 | Models and effort levels are read from each agent over ACP session config options (`model`, `thought_level`), never hardcoded. Agents can set `auto` for either | Model names and effort levels change often. ACP already exposes both | Hardcoded model lists per tool |
| 2026-09-29 | Use `@agentclientprotocol/claude-agent-acp` and `@agentclientprotocol/codex-acp` | The `@zed-industries` packages are deprecated or replaced (checked on npm, 2026-09-29) | The old Zed packages |
| 2026-09-29 | Decision provider defaults to Laya running locally, then an ACP agent simulating it, then rules. Jev stays opt-in | Owner wants Laya local and a fallback when Laya or Jev is not available | Rules only by default |
| 2026-09-29 | First workspace root is `~/Work` | Owner's answer | |
| 2026-09-29 | Every agent session has a context budget: compact at 80% of the window (ACP `usage_update`), first with the agent's native `compact` command, then by handoff to a fresh session if that fails or does not bring usage under 40%. Also rotate after 40 turns | Owner does not want context to pile up and burn tokens. block/buzz gates on provider-reported usage (its fix #821), caps compactions per turn (#4805), and rotates external ACP agents' sessions (`BUZZ_ACP_MAX_TURNS_PER_SESSION`, recommended 50). Both adapters already expose `usage_update` and a `compact` command | Let each CLI compact on its own (buzz-acp's approach, no control over when); rotation only (loses in-session understanding every time) |
| 2026-09-29 | Connections (kubectl, MCP, SSH, env, mail) per org. Org agents use their org's; root agents can use all. Secrets in `secrets.age`, injected per run, removed after | Owner debugs servers, reads observability and mail across orgs from the hub | Put credentials in agent config homes; one global set for everyone |
| 2026-09-29 | Connection writes always ask the owner unless the org policy allows that exact action; unclassified commands count as writes. The primary guard is a least-privilege credential | MCP read-only flags have been bypassed (containers/kubernetes-mcp-server #1415) and agents with a shell can call CLIs directly | Trust MCP read-only modes; parse and block commands only |
| 2026-09-29 | Task kinds `code`, `ops`, `chat`; ops tasks write `REPORT.md` and create linked fix tasks. Live control (stream, Esc to stop, queue or interrupt) in Phase 2 | majhi must replace the CLIs entirely, including debugging and reporting | Repo tasks only |
| 2026-09-29 | New Phase 4 "Connections and ops tasks"; later phases renumbered 5 to 10. `hub-tasks` moves to Phase 4 | Incident work is daily work and should not wait for the root agent phase | Fold into the root agents phase |
| 2026-09-29 | Config folder is `~/.majhi` | Matches the product name. Owner's choice | `~/.work` |
| 2026-09-29 | Tech stack and memory design approved as proposed (versions checked on npm and PyPI on 2026-09-29) | Owner's answer | |
| 2026-09-29 | Hub listens on `127.0.0.1:7070` | Owner's answer | |
| 2026-09-29 | Decision models are a tool any agent can call (`hub-decide`, on by default), plus the owner from the palette | Owner wants agents to use Jev or Laya for any quick decision, not only the hub's internal routing | Hub-internal use only |
| 2026-09-29 | One root agent is the boss (`boss:` in `hub.yaml`, changeable). It gets `hub-admin`, which exposes every command in majhi | Owner wants to set up and run everything by talking to one agent, including choosing who the boss is | Separate Setup wizard; UI-only admin |
| 2026-09-29 | One typed command layer for every change, shared by UI, palette, boss and tests | Keeps the boss and the UI equal and avoids UI-only features | Separate admin API for agents |
| 2026-09-29 | Risk classes: `read` runs; `change` runs when the owner asked for it, else proposes; `destructive` and `outbound` always confirm. Policy is owner-editable | Owner wants the boss to act freely on request but keep approval over risky and outward actions | Confirm everything (slow); confirm nothing (unsafe) |
| 2026-09-29 | `~/.majhi` is a git repo, one commit per config change, with undo | Makes a powerful boss safe to use: every change is visible and reversible | No history; manual backups |
| 2026-09-29 | Secrets pasted in chat are captured into `secrets.age` and replaced with a reference before any model sees them | The boss will handle logins and keys; secrets must not enter model context | Trust agents with raw secrets |
| 2026-09-29 | Agents start on demand, stop after 10 idle minutes, with global, per-account and per-task concurrency limits, all configurable live | Owner wants memory and resource efficiency at scale | Keep every agent process running |
| 2026-09-29 | Everything is named majhi: `majhi.yaml`, `majhi.db`, tasks folder `<first root>/.majhi`, MCP tools `majhi-*`, `MAJHI_RUNNER`, and the backend is `apps/server`. Earlier rows that say `hub.yaml` or `hub-*` refer to these | One name for the product, its files and its tools | Keep "hub" for internal pieces |
| 2026-09-29 | Phase order stays: 0 skeleton, 1 full account management (accounts, logins, API keys, per-agent homes, agents), 2 first usable (one agent end to end, live control, the boss). Every phase ships something runnable with `make up` and a demo note | Owner wants full account management before daily use | Use existing CLI logins first and move accounts later |
| 2026-09-29 | Onboarding is a guided step flow: roots (Phase 0), first account and choosing the boss (Phase 1), then the boss finishes setup as a conversation (Phase 2) | Owner wants to set up and choose the boss at the start. The boss needs an account and the ACP runtime, so its steps arrive with those phases | One big setup form; boss-only setup from day one |
| 2026-09-29 | Phase 0 runs one container, `server`, which serves the built web app. Vite runs only in dev | SPEC 4.2 allows it; fewer moving parts | Separate `web` container |
| 2026-09-29 | `POST /api/cmd/*` rejects requests whose `Origin` is not localhost, 127.0.0.1 or [::1] (403) | Without it any website the owner visits could change the config | Token auth (later, if majhi is ever exposed beyond localhost) |
| 2026-09-29 | Hand edits to `majhi.yaml` are committed on their own (`manual: changes made outside majhi`) before a command writes | Undoing a command must never undo the owner's own edits | Commit hand edits together with the next command |
| 2026-09-29 | `workspaces.set` without `tasks_dir` removes it, so the default `<first root>/.majhi` applies; the UI sends the current value to keep it | One clear meaning for "not set" | Keep the old value when the field is missing |
| 2026-09-29 | Config commits run git with signing and hooks off and `safe.directory` set to `~/.majhi` | Host signing, hooks or a different uid on the Docker mount must not break config history | Use the host's git config as is |
| 2026-09-29 | The repo list uses memoized rows and CSS `content-visibility` instead of a virtualization library | Smooth at a few hundred repos without a new dependency; revisit if lists grow past that | `@tanstack/react-virtual` now |
| 2026-09-29 | Packages published less than a day ago are not installed (pnpm release-age gate); pinned hono 4.13.9, @hono/node-server 2.1.1, motion 13.4.4 | Supply-chain safety | Exclude fresh versions from the gate |
| 2026-09-29 | SSH agent socket defaults to `/run/host-services/ssh-auth.sock`, verified to work on OrbStack | Same path as Docker Desktop, so one default fits both | Per-runtime detection |
| 2026-09-29 | A host helper (`apps/host`, native Node, macOS LaunchAgent installed by `make up`) lists host folders, suggests roots and remounts roots by recreating the server container. It connects to the server by long-polling `127.0.0.1:7070` with a token file; it opens no port. Replaces the manual second `make up` | Owner does not want to run `make up` or type paths. On OrbStack, containers cannot reach host localhost services or Unix sockets through bind mounts (both tested), so the helper dials in instead | Mount all of `$HOME` (exposes private keys and every CLI's credentials to agents); give the container the Docker socket (same exposure, and it would restart itself); keep the manual `make up` |
| 2026-09-29 | Folder picking is an in-app browser plus root suggestions, not the native Finder dialog | Keyboard-first, works the same everywhere, and fully testable. A Finder dialog from a LaunchAgent may open behind the browser and needs Automation permission | `osascript choose folder` |
| 2026-09-29 | Agents on one account share the account's config home. No per-agent home that links the credential file | Claude writes `.credentials.json` by atomic rename and keeps its refresh lock in the config dir, so a symlinked file breaks or goes stale. Codex has no lock between processes either. Per-agent skills come in Phase 7 through the prompt or the task folder (SPEC 5.2 fallback) | Symlink the credential file per agent; `claude setup-token` per agent (never refreshes) |
| 2026-09-29 | The server container is also the runner in v1: the Claude and Codex ACP adapters (with their bundled CLIs) are installed in the server image | SPEC 4.2 allows it; one container until process isolation is needed | Separate `runner` service now |
| 2026-09-29 | The age key for `secrets.age` lives outside `~/.majhi`, at `~/.config/majhi/secrets.key`, created by `make up` and passed as a Docker secret. `secrets.age` is git-ignored | The key must not sit next to the file it protects, and undoing a config commit must never bring back a removed key | Key inside `~/.majhi`; commit the encrypted file |
| 2026-09-29 | Health checks spend no tokens: the CLI starts, its own auth status command reports signed in, an ACP session opens, and the agent's model and effort are in the session's option list | Enough to catch every setup error; a real model call adds cost and rate-limit risk | A one-token prompt per check |
| 2026-09-29 | The UI gets live updates from one `/api/events` WebSocket that names changed topics; the client refetches | Hand edits and boss changes must show without reload; rooms in Phase 2 need a socket anyway | Polling; server-sent events |
| 2026-09-29 | Laya runs natively on Apple silicon Macs with `laya-mlx` through the host helper, and in the `laya` Docker service (PyTorch CPU) on Linux, Windows and Intel Macs, or as the fallback when the native one is not available | Owner's choice. MLX uses the Mac's GPU; Docker on macOS cannot, so PyTorch there runs on CPU only | Docker everywhere; native only |
| 2026-09-29 | Org agents may use their org's accounts or the owner's personal accounts, never another org's. Root agents may use any account | SPEC 6 forbids other orgs' credentials; using a personal subscription for org work is the owner's call | Org accounts only |
| 2026-09-29 | Onboarding steps 2 and 3 have "Skip for now" (remembered in the browser) | A broken account or CLI must not lock the owner out of majhi; SPEC 3.6 only allowed skipping after step 3 | No skip before the boss exists |
| 2026-09-29 | Studio opens with Cmd/Ctrl+. and closes with Esc; Esc typed in the login terminal goes to the terminal | Cmd K is the palette and Cmd J the boss (SPEC); `.` is free in every browser | Cmd+Shift+S |
| 2026-09-29 | Tasks can have a parent (nested children with progress rollup) and `depends-on` links to other tasks, in one link model with `follow-up`. A dependency is met when its MRs are merged by default, or when it reaches review (`ready`, stacked branches) per link. Waiting is a chip, not a status. Data model in Phase 2, agent-driven splitting and auto-start in Phase 4, merge-based dependencies in Phase 5 | Owner wants parent/child tasks and dependencies. Merged is the safe default; stacking is opt-in for speed. Adding the fields in Phase 2 avoids a migration | New `blocked` status; separate models per link type |
| 2026-09-29 | Account limits (5-hour and weekly) are read without model tokens: Claude through the Agent SDK's usage call, run by a small script from the adapter's own install (it starts a query that never gets a message); Codex through `codex-acp cli app-server`, `account/rateLimits/read`. Read after each passing health check and login, and every 10 minutes for signed-in login accounts. A failed read keeps the last numbers and shows the error, and never makes the account unhealthy. Risk: the Claude method is named `usage_EXPERIMENTAL_MAY_CHANGE_DO_NOT_RELY_ON_THIS_API_YET` and can change in any SDK release, and the adapter version is pinned in the Dockerfile for that reason | SPEC 3.3 wants usage in the Accounts table before any agent has run, and both tools expose it for free. The script travels as a string run with `node -e`, so it needs no file lookup in tsx, the bundle or the image | Wait for Phase 2 token counts; ask the model to run `/usage` (spends tokens); scrape the CLI screen |
| 2026-09-29 | Phase 2 ships in two parts: 2a (one agent end to end) and 2b (boss, compaction, task links UI, concurrency) | Owner wants to use majhi as soon as possible; 2a alone is useful for daily coding | One large Phase 2 |
| 2026-09-29 | A task's id is its key (`GLX-420`, `LOCAL-9`) and also its folder name. Orgs get a `key` prefix, derived from the name when not set | One id everywhere: UI, folder, branch, commits | UUIDs plus a separate display key |
| 2026-09-29 | Default agent for a new task: the @mentioned one; else agents that can work in the task's org on a usable account, preferring Lead, then Builder, then others, org-scoped before root; `LOCAL` chat tasks default to the boss. Shown as a chip the owner can change | Works before teams exist (Phase 3) | Always ask |
| 2026-09-29 | Permission requests are allowed automatically when the agent's perms cover them: reads, searches and fetches always; edits, deletes and moves with `edit`; commands with `shell`, except `git push` (needs `push`). Everything else asks the owner inline. "Allow for this task" is remembered per task and tool kind | SPEC 5.1; keeps prompts rare without hiding risky actions | Ask for everything |
| 2026-09-29 | Attachments in 2a: images go to the agent as ACP image blocks; files are stored and listed in TASK.md by path; links are fetched once and stored as text. Summaries come later (they need a model call) | Useful now without spending tokens | Summarize with an agent now |
| 2026-09-29 | The main screen becomes the task screen. The repos list moves to its own view, where projects are registered | SPEC 3.1; repos are setup, tasks are daily work | Keep repos as home |
| 2026-09-29 | `gh pr` and `glab mr` commands need the `mr` permission; an "allow for this task" choice never covers pushes or MR commands | Outbound actions stay explicit (SPEC 3.4) | Treat them as plain shell |
| 2026-09-29 | Push detection reads the command text (`git ... push`, also inside `sh -c`). A script or alias that pushes is not caught; the rule is a guard, not a sandbox | Agents run real shells. Stronger isolation (push credentials only on approval) comes with MRs in Phase 5 | Block all network from agent shells |
| 2026-09-29 | Room items display in `at` order (set once, strictly increasing per task); `seq` bumps on every write and is used for paging and upserts | Streaming updates must not reorder the room | Order by seq |
| 2026-09-29 | After Esc or Stop all, queued messages stay held until the next send or start | Stopping must stop; the owner decides when to continue | Send the queue right after cancel |
| 2026-09-29 | Sending a message to an inbox, ready or paused task starts it. If starting fails on create, the task is kept as `ready` and the error says why | Fewest clicks; nothing the owner typed is lost | Refuse sends to tasks that are not running |
| 2026-09-29 | Connections and API-key accounts use typed fields: `secret` (secrets.age), `text` (plain, visible, in majhi.yaml) and `file` (stored under connections/, path passed in a variable). One connection or account can hold several fields | Services often need a key plus ids, endpoints or a credentials file; ids are not secrets and should stay visible | Every value as a secret; one value per connection |
| 2026-09-29 | Marketing work (media in the room, ffmpeg and Remotion, video APIs, browser connections, social platform APIs, scheduled tasks) is the last phase, Phase 11 | Owner's priority: coding and ops first | Before Phase 5 |
| 2026-09-29 | Agents show media and pages by saving files in the task folder and linking them with markdown; the room renders them inline or as cards that open in a new tab. Task files are served with path containment, no dot folders, and HTML/SVG under a `sandbox` CSP so it runs in an opaque origin and cannot call the API | Owner needs to see images, videos and pages agents make. No new tool needed; works with any agent | A `share` MCP tool; iframing agent HTML inside the app |
| 2026-09-29 | Permission buttons are named by option kind: allow once is "Allow", allow always is "Allow for this task", reject once is "Deny". The adapter's own names are used for the rest | Adapters say "Always allow", but majhi only remembers the choice for one task and one tool kind | Show the adapter's names |
| 2026-09-29 | Agents show media by saving files in the task folder and linking them in markdown. The server serves the folder at `GET /api/tasks/<id>/files/<path>`: real paths only inside the folder, no dot names, `nosniff`, `no-store`, Range. HTML and SVG get `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox allow-scripts allow-forms allow-popups allow-downloads`. Command, upload and socket endpoints already refuse any Origin that is not loopback, which includes `null` | Pages should run (charts, reports) but never reach majhi's API or the owner's other files. An opaque origin does that without iframes in the app | Iframe the page; strip scripts; serve from another port |
| 2026-09-29 | ACP image blocks and resource links are kept: images are saved to `<task>/media/<n>.<ext>` (up to 8 MB, common image types) and listed in an additive `media` array on the agent item; links to files inside the task folder become files URLs, other `file://` links are dropped | The agent may draw or link something without writing a file first | Drop non-text content |
| 2026-09-29 | Web images in agent markdown are not loaded into the room; they become a link card. Only images and clips in the task folder render inline | Loading a URL an agent chose lets it track the owner and reach local services | Load them |
| 2026-09-29 | `.md` and `.markdown` files are served as `text/plain`; unknown extensions as `application/octet-stream` with `Content-Disposition: attachment` | Browsers download `text/markdown`; unknown types must not be sniffed | Serve `text/markdown` |
| 2026-09-29 | The e2e server sets `GIT_SSH_COMMAND=false` and gives the Claude and Codex fakes a 250 ms and 600 ms pause per step | Fixture remotes are made up and a fetch must fail at once without touching the network; a turn needs to last long enough to watch and stop | Let git try ssh; add a slow flag per prompt |
| 2026-09-29 | Roots settings return to Repos (`/repos`) after saving, not to the task screen | "Show repos now" and saving roots are about repos | Go home |
| 2026-09-29 | Principle: no manual work outside majhi. majhi does setup and fixes itself (UI, boss, host helper); when the owner must act outside it, majhi says so explicitly in the UI with the exact step. Terminal commands are fallbacks | Owner's rule | Documented manual steps |
| 2026-09-29 | Resume after sleep, shutdown, reboot, offline and crash moves from Phase 8 to 2b, and is automatic (no "offer resume"). Checkpoint after every turn (WIP commit plus session id). Limit handoff stays in Phase 8 | Owner closes the laptop daily; no manual work outside majhi | Keep it in Phase 8; resume on click |
| 2026-09-29 | The host helper loads SSH keys into the Mac's agent: at start (login), after every wake from sleep, when a fetch fails for lack of SSH access, and on `ssh.reload`. It runs `/usr/bin/ssh-add --apple-load-keychain`, then `ssh-add` for each key without a passphrase (found through `~/.ssh/config` `IdentityFile` entries and `id_ed25519`, `id_ecdsa`, `id_rsa`). Keys with a passphrase are reported in `host.status` (`ssh.needsPassphrase`) | The agent on this Mac was empty and majhi could not fetch. Apple's `ssh-add` is used by path because Homebrew's lacks the Keychain flags. All ssh tools run without a terminal, stdin closed, askpass off | Ask the owner to run `ssh-add` by hand; mount keys into the container (forbidden) |
| 2026-09-29 | The owner unlocks a passphrase-protected key in majhi (`ssh.unlock`, Repos screen). The passphrase goes browser to server to helper over localhost with the host token, into a 0600 file in a 0700 temp folder read by a throwaway askpass script for one `ssh-add --apple-use-keychain`, then the folder is deleted. majhi never stores or logs it; the macOS Keychain keeps it, and later reloads use `--apple-load-keychain`. The command is refused unless the key is in the helper's `needsPassphrase` list. The terminal command stays as an "or run it yourself" fallback and in `doctor` | Setup must not need a terminal (SPEC 2). Same trust as the owner typing it into the Keychain prompt: localhost only, one process hop at a time | Tell the owner to run the command; keep the passphrase in `secrets.age` |
| 2026-09-29 | Agent runs get no `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`. The server's own git (fetch when a task starts, pushes in Phase 5) still uses it. `AGENTS.md` tells the agent to ask the owner in the room to fetch or pull | An agent could push through a script that the push check (command text) does not catch. With no socket it cannot reach any SSH remote, so the earlier "a script that pushes is not caught" gap is closed for SSH remotes (HTTPS remotes have no stored credentials in the run env either). An `ssh` connection type may later give one run its own access, decided in the connections phase | Keep the socket and rely on the command check; a deny list of ssh commands |
| 2026-09-29 | Public keys are mounted read-only at their host paths: for every `IdentityFile` in `~/.ssh/config` and the default names whose `<path>.pub` exists, in the generated `docker-compose.override.yml`. The host computes the list (helper on remount, `sh scripts/host.sh pubkeys` in `make up`) and passes it as `MAJHI_SSH_PUBKEYS`, because the container cannot see which files exist and a missing bind source must not be created as a folder. `gen-override` refuses any source without `.pub`. Private keys are never mounted | `Host gitlab-acme` has `IdentitiesOnly yes` and an `IdentityFile` the container lacks, so ssh offered no agent key. OpenSSH selects the agent key from `<IdentityFile>.pub` | Mount `~/.ssh` (exposes private keys); drop `IdentitiesOnly` in a generated config |
| 2026-09-29 | The image gets a passwd entry for the owner's uid at build time (build args `HOST_UID`, `HOST_GID`, `HOST_HOME`), `/etc/passwd` stays read-only, and setuid/setgid bits are stripped from every binary | OpenSSH will not start for an unknown uid, so ssh never worked in the container. A writable passwd plus the setuid `su` would let an agent become root in the container | Entry added at start with a writable passwd (rejected: root escalation); nss_wrapper |
| 2026-09-29 | `doctor` and `host.status` probe each ssh alias or `user@host` used by a registered project's remotes with `ssh -T -o BatchMode=yes -o ConnectTimeout=5`, and keep the result five minutes. Results are fixed sentences, never ssh output | A loaded key is not proof that the host accepts it. Repos shows the host that took none | Probe on every status poll |
| 2026-09-29 | The UI follows the clickable demo (`design/Work Hub · clickable demo-html`): sidebar navigation with pages (Board, Agents, Health and usage, Skills, Hub setup, Projects and links, Orgs and accounts), a Board as home, a task view with the room and an "In this room" panel, and a New task dialog. It replaces the three-column design and the Studio overlay built in Phases 1 and 2a; data, server and behaviour stay | Owner's choice: the clickable demo is the design they want. It was not in the repo when Phases 1 and 2a were built | Keep the three-column design |
| 2026-09-29 | A running task whose agents are all idle ("your turn") stays in the Working column with a violet "Your turn" note. Paused tasks also stay in Working, with a coral "Paused · reason" note and a warm border. Your review holds only tasks in `review` status | The demo's Your review means work is finished and needs a decision; a chat that waits for a reply is still running work. The note keeps it findable, and Working sorts paused and your-turn cards first, then by id, so cards do not shuffle while agents stream | A sixth column for your turn; putting them in Your review |
| 2026-09-29 | The board's All / Local / From trackers pills and the Sync trackers button are left out until a task has a tracker source (Phase 9). Tracker names show on a card only for such tasks | Every task is local today, so the pills would show identical counts and read as broken. Done tasks are hidden from the columns; "N done, show" in the subtitle adds a Done column | Show the pills with one empty option |
| 2026-09-29 | Board cards show "Waiting on X" only once links exist in the task list data. Today `tasks.list` carries no links, so the note is not drawn; the task links UI arrives in 2b | No server change in this pass, and no per-card `tasks.get` fan-out | Fetch every task on the board |
| 2026-09-29 | The org filter is `?org=<id>` on every page. Picking an org in the sidebar goes to the Board, as in the demo; links in the sidebar and back links keep the filter. The New task dialog opens on that org's projects and preselects its only project | One global filter that survives navigation and reloads, and can be linked | Filter in local state |
| 2026-09-29 | The banner above the page shows the most urgent of: a task paused by a usage limit or an error, a permission prompt waiting in the task that is open, an account that needs sign-in or does not answer. Others are counted ("and 2 more need you"). Prompts in tasks that are not open are not known, because only the open room has a socket | Matches the demo's single strip. Knowing prompts in every task needs a server summary field, which would be a server change | A room socket per running task |
| 2026-09-29 | New task chooses projects with chips (several allowed) and composes `text` for `tasks.create` from title, details and a closing `Repos: a, b` line for chips the words do not name. Projects and the base branch named in the title or details ("fix x in api from develop") preselect their chip and are locked on, so what is shown is what the server parses. "Depends on" and "Tracking" are left out until task links (2b) and trackers (Phase 9) exist | The server's parser is the only way to set repos and base, and `tasks.create` takes no explicit fields; power users keep the one-line way | Change the server to take explicit repos |
| 2026-09-29 | Onboarding, the invalid-config screen and the server-unreachable screen replace the whole window (no sidebar). The gate now sits in the app shell, so every route is covered; `/settings/roots` stays reachable in every state | A sidebar of links that lead nowhere during first-run is noise | Gate only `/` as before |
| 2026-09-29 | Avatars take the role color of the agent (Lead violet, Builder amber, Reviewer blue, Tester pink, Root green) and the initial of the last word of the id ("globex-lead" gives L); unknown agents fall back to a stable hue. The state is the options' `id: description` lines. Each hook runs the repo's hook of the same name, from the repo's own `core.hooksPath` (husky, lefthook) read with `git config --local`, else from its git folder, and `prepare-commit-msg` adds the trailer, which runs even under `--no-verify`. An agent's own commits use one run environment for every repo of the task, so a run turns attribution off when any project in its task has it off, and a change applies to runs that start after it. (1) A background process's end wakes only the agent that started it, once: it is stored as a room item with id `process-end:<id>:<startedAt>`, so a replayed end wakes nobody, also after a restart (no migration). An end that a newer run of the same name or command (in the same task, started later; a restart counts) has replaced is a quiet line. Ends that wait for an agent (busy or paused) go out in one prompt, newest first, without the ones replaced by then, and the prompt says to reply only if the result changes what the agent does and not to mention agents just to report status. (2) A message that changed no files wakes a mentioned teammate only when it asks it to act. Plain status (`statusOnly`: waiting, "is running", "will report", with no request words) wakes nobody without asking a model; otherwise the decision chain gets one yes/no per mentioned agent (`use: routing`, logged with an outcome), and only a sure "no" at least 0.5 over chance (stricter than the 0.2 gate) keeps the agent asleep; unsure, rules or no provider wake it as before. Owner mentions, agents that just joined, the `mention` tool and turns that changed files are unaffected. Labeled eval (`rooms/eval/mention-eval.ts`, 19 cases) on Laya: 10/10 "no" kept asleep, 0 false "no" on 9 clear requests; at the plain 0.2 gate Laya gave 2 false "no"s (lift 0.22, 0.26), so the stricter bar is what makes the "no" path safe. | Rooms went in circles: old process results arrived late after an outage, and status lines with @mentions woke agents who replied with more status | A migration for delivered notices (the room item already persists and is idempotent); folding the notices into the resume prompt; ask the model for every mention, even plain status; act on a "no" at the normal gate (2 false "no"s in the eval); only the words, no model (misses thanks and "already done" replies) |

| 2026-10-02 | Queued owner messages get Send now and Remove. Send now (`room.sendNow`) moves the message to the front of each queue it waits in and stops the agent's current turn; a session still opening is not cancelled (a cancel before the first prompt held the whole queue, that message too), the message just goes first. Remove (`room.unqueue`) takes it out of every queue and keeps the item in the room as `removed`, struck through, so the log stays whole. Both are refused once the message was sent. A cut turn that resumes still continues first, before queued messages: they were meant for the next turn, and Send now is the way to put one ahead. Queued and removed owner messages stay out of the room lines a fresh session or handoff carries, so the agent does not read a queued message twice. Resume while an agent is in its turn says so in the room and on the paused card. Live state carries `turnAt` | In a real task two queued messages showed "Queued" for minutes after a Resume with no reason and no way to push them | Deleting a removed item (clients only get upserts); sending queued messages before the resume prompt (changes what a resumed turn means) |

The result goes to migration 113's `e2e_runs`, shows on Health, and lands as one line in the merging task's room. | 2026-10-03 | Skills backend (Phase 6, Part A). Installed skills live in `~/.majhi/skills/<name>/` with `skills-lock.json` beside them (the CLI's lock style: source, sourceType, ref, commit, computedHash, plus installedAt). `skills.install` and `skills.update` are two calls: the first runs the pinned `skills@1.7.0` (`add <source> -y --copy --agent claude-code codex [--skill <name>]`, `DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1`, a throwaway HOME and working folder under `<tasks dir>/.skills/`, through the runner spawner) and returns a preview with a `previewId` (30 minutes, used once, an agent can confirm only its own); the second, with `confirm`, moves exactly that into the store. Installing never enables. Enabling edits the agent file's `skills` list; `skills.remove` also takes the skill off every agent that lists it. A run gets read-only copies of only its agent's enabled skills in `<majhi home>/run/connections/skills-<id>/` (a place the mount guard already allows read-only), the session's first prompt lists each name, description and SKILL.md path, and the folder goes when the session ends. Nothing goes in a shared `.claude/skills`, because agents on one account share its home. Search uses the skills.sh JSON API (`GET /api/search?q=`), since `skills find` is interactive. A local folder source must lie inside a workspace root or the tasks folder; a zip comes through `POST /api/uploads`, is extracted with a path check (no `..`, absolute paths, backslashes, symlinks, zip64 or encryption; entry count and size capped) and installed from that folder. Links inside a skill folder are refused unless they point at a file inside it. A private https repo uses the org's saved git token as an `http.<host>.extraheader` in the throwaway run's environment, never in an argument or file; ssh sources use no login. Recorded and audited sources drop a URL's user name, password and query. Audit rows (`skill-install`, `skill-update`, `skill-enable`, `skill-disable`, `skill-remove`) carry the task of the caller, or an empty task for the owner's page | Agents of one account share a config home, so per-agent skills can only come through the run; one preview-then-commit path serves the page, the captain and the room; the store is the only copy the CLI never touches | Writing into `.claude/skills` of the account home; a `skills.confirm` command; parsing `skills find`; one lock entry per repo |
| 2026-10-03 | MCP install backend (Phase 6, Part B). An installed MCP server is an `mcp` connection (Phase 10), with no second store. `mcp.install` is two calls like skills: the first builds a preview (source, publisher, repo, transport, command or URL, headers, env, inputs, warnings) and keeps it 30 minutes in memory; `{confirm: previewId}` creates the connection, writes an `mcp-install` audit row and, when nothing is unset, runs Test and returns its tool list. Sources: the official registry (`/v0.1/servers`, falling back to `/v0`), a remote URL, a local command, or a pasted `mcpServers` snippet. From `server.json`: remotes (streamable-http or SSE) and packages (npm, pypi, oci over stdio) only; the version is always pinned (an unpinned package is refused, an unpinned local command only warns); `isRequired` inputs block confirm until `values` supplies them. Secrets are never accepted by `mcp.install`: `isSecret` entries and any secret-looking value from a snippet or header are created unset (value dropped) and returned as `needs`, and the owner fills them through `connections.setSecret`; optional secrets are left out because an unset entry makes Test refuse. A URL with credentials or a key in its query is refused. SSE is a new `protocol` field on the `mcp` type (also in the ACP server spec and Test). Enabling is `mcp.enable` / `mcp.disable`, which edit the agent's `connections` list through `agents.edit` (audit `mcp-enable`, `mcp-disable`); it is refused for root agents (their connections do not follow their own list) and across orgs. Nothing is enabled by installing. Any change to an agent's `skills` or `connections` (including removing a connection or skill) now calls `RunManager.remountAgent`, so open sessions restart and the next turn has the new mounts and MCP tools | The gap in the first cut was that an open session never saw a new skill or server. Restart goes through the existing `remount` (idle: closed now; busy: when the turn ends) | Letting agents pass secrets in the install call (leaks into the room and logs); a second MCP store (two sources of truth); auto-enabling for every agent (breaks org and agent scoping); remounting only from the skill and MCP commands (misses the agent editor and connection removal). Not exercised here: the real registry and the runner image |
| 2026-10-03 | Install by message (Phase 6, Part D). In a room, the owner's message "@agent install this skill <link>" or "@agent add this MCP server <registry name, URL, command or snippet>" is read by `parseInstallRequest` and answered with one approval card instead of a turn of the agent. Only a message that is nothing but the request counts (after leading @mentions); anything longer, like "install the skill, then fix the bug", goes to the agent as before. The server builds the preview itself as the owner and the card shows what, source, publisher, transport and which agent; approving runs `skills.install` or `mcp.install` with `{confirm, enable: <agent>}` as the owner, so there is one approval, not preview then confirm through the agent's own approval. `enable` is new on both commands, only valid with `confirm`, turns the result on for that one agent only, and writes the usual `*-install` and `*-enable` rows. The agent is the first @mentioned teammate, else the lead; it must be on the task. An org agent gets the server in its own org; a root agent has no org of its own, so the server goes to the task's org and is not turned on for it. | 2026-10-04 | Staffing scores each allowed agent from facts only: a free slot on its account (weight 3), usage left above the floors (3), expected cost against the price table and the budget left (2, 3 with a budget or an API key), model tier against task size (2), skill words found in the task text (1), and past results in the repo (2, only when task records exist). | 2026-10-04 | Usage limits per CLI (Phase 7, SPEC 5.7). Each tool's registry entry carries `limitShapes`: `error` patterns, checked on a failed prompt's error and its ACP `data`, and `line` patterns, checked on the first line of a failed turn's text and on a turn that ended normally only when its whole text is one short line (at most 400 characters) in the CLI's own form. Claude Code: "Claude AI usage limit reached\|<epoch>", "... limit reached ∙ resets 3pm", "You've hit your limit · resets 3pm (Zone)", `rate_limit_error`, 429, "would exceed the rate limit", "credit balance is too low". Codex: "You've hit your usage limit ... try again at 3:40 PM" or "in 2 days 3 hours", `usage_limit_reached`, "Rate limit reached for ... try again in 20s", `insufficient_quota`, "exceeded your current quota", 429. Context-window errors and overloads (529) never count. On API-key accounts rate-limit and quota errors are limits too | One place per CLI to add a new wording, with tests per shape. A whole-text rule keeps an agent's prose about rate limits from pausing it | One shared regex list for every tool (the old start-failure check); reading the CLIs' logs |
| 2026-10-04 | Measured and estimated, per CLI, for limits (SPEC 5.8). The reset is measured when the CLI's message names it (an epoch, a clock time with or without a zone, "in N units"); then `AccountLimit.resetKnown` is true. Otherwise it is estimated: the latest reset of a full 5-hour or weekly window from the last usage read (Claude and Codex login accounts, DECISIONS 2026-09-29), else 15 minutes from the error. The UI says "until about 4:24 PM" for an estimate. API-key accounts have no windows, so without "try again in" their reset is always the 15 minute guess. A second error while a mark holds keeps the mark's end unless it names a reset | A wrong guess costs one failed prompt, which spends no tokens, and is marked again | Ask the CLI for usage at every error (slow, and API keys have none); a fixed hour |
| 2026-10-04 | A limit marks the account, not the agent: `limit` in the account cache, `at-limit` until `until`, cleared by the 60 second budget lift sweep. Every other agent on the account hands off to its own fallback or is held at its next turn boundary (the run manager's `accountLimit` check, then the `limited` gate budgets use), and lifts through the same sweep, so there is no timer per reset. `resume.auto` gates the lift of runs in memory; the room then says once that the limit reset. After a majhi restart a task paused at a limit lifts at the reset whatever `resume.auto` says, because the stored pause does not say whether a budget or an account limit set it | One pause path for budgets, autonomy caps and account limits. A sweep survives sleep and clock changes, a timer does not | A timer per account at its reset; storing the cause of each pause (a migration for one edge case) |
| 2026-10-04 | Fallback handoff at a limit (SPEC 5.7). When the agent has a `fallback` that may work in the task's org, is not on the team yet, whose account is signed in, not at a limit and not held by a budget, and `resume.handoff` is on (majhi, org override, default on), the fallback takes the agent's place in the team at the same position, so a lead's fallback becomes the lead. It keeps only the agent's `repos` override, not its model or effort, which may not exist on the other account. It starts a fresh session from the note majhi builds from TASK.md, the last checkpoint, the room since then and the diff (the agent cannot write one: its account is out), and gets the agent's queued owner and handoff messages. The agent's session ends and it does not come back at the reset. Room line: "@acme-lead hit its usage limit (resets 4:24 PM). @acme-builder continues from the checkpoint." A fallback already on the team does not take over; the run pauses for the reset | SPEC 5.7 says hand off and resume from the checkpoint. Swapping in place keeps the lead role and the routing simple | Add the fallback beside the agent; hand back at the reset (two agents racing on one worktree); give the step to any teammate |

It hides under 1200 px like the spend line. The reset times and every account's windows are in its tooltip, and it opens Health and usage. The sidebar's Agents now and the paused cards keep their limit notes, which now take the reset from a run's limit error first ("At limit, back about 16:24" when guessed). With the reset times it no longer fit | A meter per account in the top bar; the reset in the readout itself; only the sidebar notes |
| 2026-10-04 | Migration 125 sets `paused_by = 'autonomy-off'` on tasks that are paused with reason owner, have no `paused_by`, are in `autonomy_tasks`, and whose `updated_at` is within two minutes of an `autonomy_events` mode line starting "Turned off" or "Stopping after". Stop now writes its line just after pausing, a graceful stop just before, so the window is two minutes either side. | Old rows paused by the switch before `paused_by` existed read as the owner's, and the captain never resumed them. | An exact event match (the events do not name tasks); marking every owner-paused autonomous task (would take over real owner pauses). |
| 2026-10-04 | The captain resumes with `tasks.start` on a paused task; there is no `tasks.resume`. majhi refuses the lane's call (`pickRefusal`) when the task is owner-paused (reason owner with no `paused_by`), blocked or looping, or when its cause remains: a budget hold or an at-limit account for `limit`, a signed-out account for `signed-out` or `error`, an unreachable one for `offline`. `paused_by` captain or autonomy-off always passes. | One verb the policy, authority row, slots and repo rule already cover; the refusal is structural, not only in the preamble. | A second command with the same checks; trusting the preamble. |

| 2026-10-04 | Connect, step 2 reuses the step 1 engine for provider OAuth (`provider-oauth.ts`, fetch only): PKCE S256, single-use state, `iss` check (exact, or by prefix for Microsoft; required where the provider always sends it), the client secret only in the token request body, one renewal at a time, revoke when the provider has an endpoint. | 2026-10-04 | Slack is not run as OAuth: Slack takes https redirect URLs only (docs.slack.dev, installing with OAuth), so majhi cannot use `http://127.0.0.1`. The guided setup generates the manifest (Socket Mode on, the pack's bot scopes) with a create-from-manifest deep link, and the owner pastes the bot token and the app-level token once in secure inputs; majhi checks them with `auth.test` and `apps.connections.open` and stores them as secret entries of an `env` connection (`SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`, `SLACK_APP_TOKEN`). Disconnect calls `auth.revoke`. Discord is the same shape (application ID and bot token, checked against `/users/@me`, add-the-bot link with permission bits for the access); Disconnect cannot revoke a Discord token and says so. This departs from the brief's "then the normal Connect consent" for these two. | The honest minimum Slack and Discord allow; no tunnel or hosted redirect is added. | A hosted https redirect page owned by the project (a public service to run); a Cloudflare tunnel per owner. |
| 2026-10-04 | Command-line tool sign-ins (wrangler, vercel, stripe, aws, gcloud, sentry) are `cli` connections whose folder is `~/.majhi/connections/<id>/profile`, which the runner's mount check already allows read-write for browser profiles. The helper (`apps/host/src/cliTools.ts`, jobs `cli.login`, `cli.loginCancel`, `cli.check`, `cli.logout`) moves the old profile aside, runs the login in the same path with a built environment, keeps the new one only when the tool's check command works and (on a reconnect) names the expected account, else restores the old one. A run gets only the tool's own variables (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, `AWS_CONFIG_FILE`, `CLOUDSDK_CONFIG`, ...), never HOME. The command lines are from each tool's documentation and were not run against real installs; they are data in `packages/shared/src/cli-tools.ts`. Known limit: aws single sign-on keeps its token cache under HOME, so a run sees the config files but not that cache until a later step exports credentials. | Reuses the isolation already proven for gh and glab, and the mount rule, so no new path is ever mounted; two workspaces can be signed in as different accounts because each connection has its own folder. | The runner image running the login (loopback tools cannot be reached from the browser there); copying the owner's own CLI config (identity leak). |

| 2026-10-04 | Bound all task services/previews with `containers.total` (default 8), and preview builds with `containers.build_total` (default 1), reserving before allocation. Repeated identical same-task service starts reuse the running process. On pause, retain only services without saved volumes rather than the whole stack. | Per-task caps alone grow with task count. | 2026-10-04 | Disk in done tasks is freed by the existing Cleanup chore (a code-only step added to it), not a new chore: it already runs while Autonomous is Off and once a day, and a new chore would need its own switch, cap and row. Settings `cleanup.free_after_hours` (24) and `cleanup.worktree_after_days` (7, 0 off). Task folders of tasks that no longer have a row are left alone: majhi never deletes a task row, so an orphan folder is not majhi's to judge. A done task's whole worktree goes only when it is clean even of untracked files and its tip is in the base or a remote branch; the branch always stays. Hard-linked files shared with a package store are not counted as freed. | The 81 GB task root came from 109 done tasks that kept node_modules and a per-task `.pnpm-store`. Deleting only rebuildable, untracked folders cannot lose work; the checks (tracked files, status in every repo of the task, symlinks, a recheck before each folder) keep it that way. | A separate Upkeep playbook (more machinery); deleting the whole task folder (loses TASK.md and attachments). |
| 2026-10-04 | Each workspace has one package store, `~/.majhi/cache/<org>/{pnpm-store,npm,yarn,pip}`, mounted read-write into that workspace's runs and background processes only, with five variables set by majhi (never copied from its own environment). The mount check allows exactly a folder directly under `cache` with a valid org id, read-write; `cache` itself, a subfolder, a read-only mount and a symlink are refused. The owner's task terminal gets none (its environment is minimal on purpose). Without an explicit `store-dir`, pnpm puts `.pnpm-store` inside the project when the home is on another filesystem; an explicit one ends that, and across filesystems pnpm copies instead of linking. | Hard links save the per-task copy of every package; one store per workspace keeps clients apart. | One store for all workspaces (a cross-client mount); a Docker volume (invisible to the host cleanup and to backups). |
| 2026-10-04 | Watch anything adds its own `watches` and `watch_samples` tables (migration 144) instead of widening `ops_services` and `ops_samples`, and raises its incidents through the ops watch (a check kind `watch`, key `watch:<id>`, no schema change to `ops_incidents`). | 2026-10-04 | Databases and Redis hang off a Variables (`env`) connection that holds a `DATABASE_URL`, `MYSQL_URL` or `REDIS_URL` variable; servers off an SSH connection. Redis is a small RESP client that can only send INFO, DBSIZE and LLEN. If the program is not installed the watch reads "unknown: psql is not installed on this machine". | No database driver is in the lockfile and installs are offline; the CLIs are the same approach connections already use for tests, and their read-only session is a second guard behind the statement check. | Adding `pg` and `mysql2` (a dependency and the secret in process memory for longer); a runner container for every look (slow, and a container for a number). |

No fix drops, deletes or truncates data. The undo step after a failed fix is "nothing to undo, page the owner", said on the timeline, because cancelling a query and restarting a service leave nothing to reverse. | The owner asked for safe fixes with approval; free-form commands cannot be proven safe. | Letting the captain run any fix (no proof it never destroys); an undo that re-runs a rollback (unverifiable). |

| 2026-10-04 | Every built-in playbook carries outcome rules ("When it finds something"), each a switch read at the point of action: chores through `Workspace.rulesOff`, sensors through `RulesContext.rulesOff` and the reporter, the hand-off, project cards, memory curator, ops watch and morning brief through one late-bound `ruleOff(org, id)`. A rule can start off (`default: false`): the code-health "propose a fix task" and the follow-ups "propose a task" rules. A proposed task is the finding made a task in the inbox (`findings.toTask`), never started. "Tell me in the brief" hides a playbook's findings from the brief counts only, not from Needs you. A captain playbook with every rule off is not woken; its wake lists the rules on and off. Rule ids keep their old names where a rule already existed (`ship-notready` now means not committed, a card waits or a secret). | The owner asked for a switch that really changes behaviour on every playbook. | A rules table apart from the playbooks (two places to keep in step); switches that only change wording. |
| 2026-10-04 | Automations are folded into Playbooks (schedules) and Watch (file, folder and URL triggers). A schedule becomes an owner-made clock playbook (runner `action`) whose id is the schedule's own id, so its `automation_runs` history stays linked with no copy. Its definition is a `playbook_custom` row with a `clock` block and its switch, next run and last run are `playbook_state`; the existing scheduler, now reading those rows, runs it with the same action runner and overlap rule. `schedules.*` stay as thin aliases. A path or URL trigger becomes a watch (`path` check, or price text mode) with its action as `fire.run`, alert off, and its runs re-keyed to the watch. The old rows are kept and marked `migrated_to`; the copy is one transaction and safe to run again. | One place for clock work (Playbooks) and one for looking (Watch). Reusing the scheduler and the action runner moves no behaviour and no guard. | A new playbook scheduler beside the old one (two clocks to keep equal); copying run history (duplicates); deleting the old rows (no way back). |
| 2026-10-04 | Trigger kinds Watch cannot check yet (task status, merge request, branch, process exit, usage, command output) are not migrated: they keep running on the old trigger engine, unmarked, and the `triggers.*` commands still manage them, but the Automations page that showed them is gone. Cooldown and settle time of a migrated trigger are dropped: a watch looks every N minutes (at least 1) and fires once per change. A migrated URL watch compares the page's main text, not the raw bytes, so its first look sets a new baseline. | The owner asked for file and URL triggers in Watch; the rest had no home and deleting them would lose work. | Dropping those triggers; building six more Watch checks now. |
| 2026-10-04 | The six trigger kinds Watch could not check are Watch kinds now: `task` (a task gets to done, failed or needs you), `mr` (opened, merged, failed checks, or any change), `branch` (new commits), `process` (exits, or exits with an error), `usage` (cost or tokens today, this week or this month, alerted with above) and `command` (runs in a task's sandbox, alerts on a change or on text). All remaining triggers moved to watches (`trg-x` becomes `wch-x`, runs re-keyed, old rows marked `migrated_to`), carrying their settle time and cooldown as the watch options `fire.settleMin` and `fire.cooldownMin` (minutes, rounded up). The trigger engine is gone. `triggers.*` stay as aliases that make and change watches with an action, and a `trg-` id finds its watch. Task, merge request and process watches fire when a subject joins the matching set, not when it leaves; a usage watch that was over stays over on migration. A command watch never shows or stores output, only `exit N` and a hash. Merge request "approved" is not a kind: majhi's MR record has no approval state. The planner reads task, merge request, process and usage sentences, and `then start a task to ...` becomes the watch's action; branch and command watches are made in the form. | One page for everything majhi looks at, with the same actions and guards as before (same-workspace targets, secret refusal, overlap rule, sandbox for commands). | Keeping the old engine beside Watch; dropping settle and cooldown. |
| 2026-10-04 | MR review state is one optional `review` object on the MR record, stored as JSON in `task_repos.mr_review` (migration 146), read in the same poll as state and CI; a failed review read never fails the poll. ETags are kept in memory per host client (a restart asks in full), not in the database. GitLab's unresolved blocking discussion is shown as changes requested, because its REST API has no requested-changes state; GitHub's required approval count is not read (it needs branch protection rights), so `approved` there means at least one approval and no open change request. Bitbucket takes its review state from the pull request it already reads. | The poll stays one pass per minute and the merge card needs one short line. A stored ETag would outlive a changed review and needs a column for no gain; the 304 saves rate limit between polls. | A separate review poller (a second cadence and rate budget); a column per field; GitHub GraphQL `reviewDecision` (no ETag, and it needs the same protection rights). This replaces the earlier note that MR "approved" is not a Watch kind. |
| 2026-10-04 | Percent limit watches extend the `usage` check with a `source` instead of a new kind, and add the conditions `atLimit` and `resets`. `resets` is edge based (a look at the limit arms it, the next look under it fires and disarms), so it needs no clock and survives a stale cached window. A watch in workspace X may read an account of X or a `private` one, and the workspace's own budget; another workspace's account is refused when saved and again at every look. `tasks.resume` is a watch action, not a command: it resumes only the workspace's `limit`-paused tasks that no budget or Auto-pilot hold still keeps, as majhi (not as an owner resume), like the budget lift. | Reuses the usage row, form and incident path; an edge needs one flag. Guards match the budget and account isolation rules. | A new kind per limit; reading the reset time to schedule the firing (breaks when the window moves); resuming every paused task (could undo the owner's own pause). |
| 2026-10-04 | A connection reaches every agent of its workspace and every root agent, minus the agents in its `agents_off`. The agent file's `connections` list no longer decides connections; `mcp.enable` and `mcp.disable` switch `agents_off`. Connect allows several connections of one service per workspace, named by the owner. DigitalOcean is one catalog entry with `products`; its connection stores the picked ones, and each becomes an MCP server on the run with the same token. | No agent listed any connection, so connected services reached no agent; the owner wants several accounts of one service and one DigitalOcean sign-in. | Keep per-agent lists and fill them at connect time (two places to keep in step); one connection per DigitalOcean product (one sign-in each). |
| 2026-10-04 | Owner-only commands that do not raise an agent's own power, spend limit or audit now take agents through the approval policy like any change: playbooks and goals, findings' proposals and deadlines, watched services and incident acks, the agenda (captain only, a lane sees its own workspace and no brief), and fast-forwarding a target branch. Each is scoped to the caller's own workspace; the captain outside a lane works for any. Still the owner's: autonomy and captain switches, trust, money and scorecard settings, answering decisions, a channel's outbound mode and sending drafts, the phone push and escalation settings, git sign-in and saved logins, secret values, turning protection off, shipping a protected repo, and in a playbook a chore's daily limit, turning an outcome rule on, or a clock that resumes tasks a limit paused. A playbook limit is refused before a card is posted. In autonomous mode a loosened change follows the existing verdict table, so where the owner set Approvals to "decide" the captain's own playbook change is approved without the owner's click. | The owner wants the captain to do these through normal approvals; the line between kept and loosened is whether a call widens the agent's own leash. | Keeping them owner-only (the captain cannot act even after approval); a per-command exception list in the autonomy verdict table (stricter than the normal path the owner asked for). |
| 2026-10-04 | Destructive actions always wait for the owner's own click. The connection gate marks a write `destructive` when its action text (command line or MCP tool name, paths and URLs left out) has a word that deletes or destroys (delete, del, destroy, drop, dropdb, purge, terminate, truncate, rm, rmi, rmdir, remove, wipe, erase, prune, uninstall, shred, mkfs, flushall, flushdb), or is a forced push or `reset --hard`. Such a write is never `allowed` by the connection's `allow`, its prompt has no Allow always, and the captain cannot approve it (it may still reject). For majhi's own commands, a destructive command is always `confirm` whatever the policy modes, no always-allow rule covers it, and the `allow_destructive_rules` switch is gone (the yaml key still loads and is ignored) | The owner requires that deletes never run without their click, whatever allow lists, task allowances, approval rules or a captain full-access switch say. MongoDB checks are a JSON command from an allow list (count, dbStats, collStats, serverStatus) with a dotted path to the number; code and write operators ($where, $function, $out, $merge) are refused anywhere in a filter. Any other database uses engine `image`: the database's official image run once in a throwaway container (`majhi-dbcheck-*`, `--rm`, read-only root, no mount, no published port, limits, the connection's values as env for that run), checked by `assertSafe`; its read-only guarantee is the owner's read-only login plus a refused list of write words and no shell as the program. A name is always prefixed with the caller's key and an id is accepted only after its label says it is the task's own, so no call reaches another task. Slug: lowercase ASCII, at most 40 characters, cut between words. ## 2026-10-04 Machine-wide run cap

Decision: `limits.runs_total` caps agent runs on the whole machine, helpers included. Default max(2, floor(cores / 3)), 3 without a machine reading, never above `agents_max`. It reuses the existing slot queue (owner first, then request order), so it applies to every start path. After a restart, cut runs come back one every 20 seconds, only while a run is free and the machine is not busy. A run container at 95% of its memory limit for two readings gets one warning on its task.
Reason: ten runner containers started in one minute after an update and the laptop crawled.
Alternatives: a second queue beside the slot queue (rejected, two lines to read); raising container memory automatically (out of scope).
| 2026-10-04 | Ship checks never give up on a diff's size. The secret scan reads what the branch adds over its merge base with its base branch (not the recorded start commit, which includes anything main brought in), one file at a time, leaving out lockfiles, generated output and binaries by pattern. It stops only past 5,000 files or 3,000,000 added lines, and then names the three biggest files. A failed hand-off check (tests, build, lint) runs again when majhi's commit or the runner image changed since it failed, or after 6 hours, at most 3 attempts for one state of the work. Hand-off checks get their own caps: `containers.handoff_cpus` (default half the cores, at least 2), `containers.handoff_memory` (default 4g) and `containers.handoff_minutes.<project>` (default 10 for tests and build); a timeout line names them. Uncommitted work in a task in review sends the lead "Commit or discard the uncommitted changes in <repo>: <files>" once per state of the work, twice at most. | The captain's log showed tasks stuck on "too large to check for secrets", on checks that failed because of a missing docker shim the update had since added, on a 600 s timeout at one CPU, and on uncommitted files nobody was told about. | Skip the scan past a size (a secret could hide in a big diff); retry failures forever (loops on real failures); one global timeout (a project's suite differs from another's). |

It never returns, posts or logs the value, refuses a name that exists, a request of another workspace and a connection the workspace does not hold; the script is shown in the room and kept in the audit log. `majhi_secrets_withdrawRequest` withdraws a pending request of the lane. The digest lists the workspace's pending secret requests with this instruction, and Tidy wakes the lane once per set of requests and withdraws a later request for the same secret name as an older pending one. Not hard-coded to any service. | The owner pasted values the captain could fetch with doctl. A review task that cannot be woken takes `runPaused(error)`, since `runLost` is for `running` only. `pausedByRuns` and `resumedByRuns` are `apply(runPaused)` and `apply(runResumed)`; a task already paused is left alone as before. | The design's run versus task rule; the reconcile tests stay green. | A `runLost` for review tasks (it would fire for every task in review). |

A binary an agent could rewrite in the run-writable tools folder must never run there. | Mount the run-writable folder in script containers; install inside the sandbox. |
| 2026-10-05 | A script declares `network: off` to run with no network, and the "only reads" text guard applies only to scripts with network. | 2026-10-05 | Hand-off checks: `defaultHandoffCpus` is `max(2, floor(cores/6))` (was half the cores), and the checks that run at once are `clamp(floor(cores/3 / cpus), 1, 2)` (was 2). On 10 cores: 1 check of 2 CPUs (20%). Hand-off containers also get `--cpu-shares 256` (Docker's default is 1024), so agent runs and the server win any contention. `containers.handoff_cpus` still overrides the CPUs. No `nice`: Docker has no nice flag, and shares are the lever the runner's args allow. | Two checks of half the cores took the machine (384% CPU, load 8 on 10 cores) while Auto-pilot was on. A check is short; waiting a little is cheaper than a slow server. | `--cpus` alone (a hard cap does nothing to a busy-looking machine below it); `nice` inside the container (needs the check's command wrapped, and cannot lower the container's weight). |

The server reads it from `MAJHI_WEB_DIST/index.html`, reports it on `/health` (`build`) and in a `hello` first frame on `/api/events` (no `seq`; old tabs drop unknown frames). A tab with a different id reloads at once when no textarea holds text, else shows a bar and reloads when the text is gone; at most once a minute per server build, so a stale cache cannot loop. Pure rule: `reloadDecision` in `packages/shared`. | The commit would put `MAJHI_COMMIT` into the web build stage and miss the Docker cache on every commit (7 GB per deploy). The file hash changes exactly when the bundle does. | The commit as the id (cache cost; reloads tabs for a docs-only commit). |
| 2026-10-05 | Start gates in the digest: `startGate` (machine busy, workspace at tasks-at-once, no free agent slot) is typed (`kind`, `text`, `key`). The wake digest names the gate on every backlog task and resumable paused task it holds, the gates are part of `factsOf`, and a task in `roomWait` counts as an explained stall (no stall wake). A refused resume says its line once, not every minute sweep. A refusal writes events only and never wakes the captain, so the same gate in the same state is the same fact: no new wake until it changes. | 2026-10-05 | macOS notifications come from majhi's own notifier app, never from osascript. The helper builds `<MAJHI_HOME>/bin/majhi-notifier/majhi.app` on the owner's Mac when it starts: a small Swift program (`apps/host/src/platform/majhiNotifierSource.ts`) compiled with `swiftc` (needs the Command Line Tools, checked with `xcode-select -p`), the majhi mark as its icon (built with `sips` and `iconutil` from an embedded PNG), bundle id `dev.majhi.alerts`, name `majhi`, signed ad hoc (`codesign --sign -`), then verified (`codesign --verify --strict`, and the program runs `status`) before it is renamed into place. It posts through UNUserNotificationCenter, carries the page URL in the notification, and opens it when macOS starts the app again on a click. A newer helper rebuilds an older app (a stamp of the source is in its Info.plist). Where it cannot be built (no Command Line Tools, or the build fails) the helper falls back to terminal-notifier as before. Results are typed from the exit code, never the text: `shown`, `blocked` (exit 3, or 5 for "owner not asked yet"), `unavailable` (no notifier yet), `failed`. Blocked raises one Needs you item of a new kind `notifications` (label Access), id `notify:mac`: "Mac notifications are off for majhi", with "Open Notification settings" (the helper runs `open x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.Notifications-Settings.extension`) and "Check again" (sends a test; the item clears when it shows). The question macOS asks for permission is asked by `majhi-notifier authorize`, which the helper starts at launch and never ends early: a notifier that is stopped while the question is on screen counts as a No for good, and `tccutil reset UserNotification` does not undo it on this macOS. That is what happened to terminal-notifier (its first post ran under a 10 s timeout). | osascript's notifications come from Script Editor and a click opens Script Editor, so a silent fallback showed the wrong name and a dead click. An app of our own is the only way to get the name majhi, the icon and the click together: terminal-notifier's `-appIcon` and `-contentImage` only change the picture inside the banner, and its name stays terminal-notifier unless the bundle is renamed and re-signed, which means keeping a patched copy of someone else's binary. Building from source on the Mac needs no developer account, no download and no stored binary. | Renamed and re-signed terminal-notifier (a patched third-party binary, still needs the same permission dance); bundling a prebuilt majhi.app in the repo (a binary in git, signed ad hoc elsewhere); osascript as a fallback (wrong name, dead click). |

A workspace switch also sets an opt-out on the agents already there (so one that lists the skill is off too) and stores the workspace rule, so agents made later in that workspace follow it. | The owner wants Enable all and Disable all per workspace, and a workspace that is off to stay off for new agents.
